**A. `packages/server/src/orchestrator/json-stream.ts`** (new)
```ts
export interface TextFieldDecoder {
  push(chunk: string): string;
  finish(): string;
  /** 已经进入 text 字段的值区 */
  readonly inside: boolean;
}
export function createTextFieldDecoder(field = 'text'): TextFieldDecoder
```
Implementation details:
- phase: 'seek' | 'colon' | 'open' | 'value' | 'done'
- buffer string for seek/colon/open.
- In 'value': process chars with pendingEscape state; handle \uXXXX via 4-hex accumulation.

Let me write it carefully.

```ts
export function createTextFieldDecoder(field: string = 'text'): TextFieldDecoder {
  const key = '"' + field + '"';
  let phase: 'seek' | 'colon' | 'open' | 'value' | 'done' = 'seek';
  let buffer = '';
  let escape = false;
  let unicode = '';
  let out = '';

  function push(chunk: string): string {
    let produced = '';
    for (const ch of chunk) {
      if (phase === 'value') {
        if (unicode !== '') {
          unicode += ch;
          if (unicode.length === 4) {
            const code = parseInt(unicode, 16);
            if (Number.isFinite(code)) produced += String.fromCharCode(code);
            unicode = '';
          }
          continue;
        }
        if (escape) {
          escape = false;
          if (ch === 'n') produced += '\n';
          else if (ch === 't') produced += '\t';
          else if (ch === 'r') produced += '\r';
          else if (ch === 'b') produced += '\b';
          else if (ch === 'f') produced += '\f';
          else if (ch === 'u') unicode = '';
          else produced += ch;
          continue;
        }
        if (ch === '\\') { escape = true; continue; }
        if (ch === '"') { phase = 'done'; continue; }
        produced += ch;
        continue;
      }
      if (phase === 'done') continue;
      // seek / colon / open
      buffer += ch;
      if (phase === 'seek') {
        if (buffer.endsWith(key)) { phase = 'colon'; buffer = ''; }
        else if (buffer.length > key.length) buffer = buffer.slice(-key.length);
        continue;
      }
      if (phase === 'colon') {
        if (ch === ' ' || ch === '\n' || ch === '\r' || ch === '\t') continue;
        if (ch === ':') { phase = 'open'; continue; }
        // 不是我们等的形状：退回搜索
        phase = 'seek'; buffer = ''; continue;
      }
      // open
      if (ch === ' ' || ch === '\n' || ch === '\r' || ch === '\t') continue;
      if (ch === '"') { phase = 'value'; buffer=''; continue; }
      phase = 'seek'; buffer = '';
    }
    return produced;
  }
  ...
}
```
Wait: in 'seek' phase, when buffer ends with key we set phase colon. But the check `buffer.endsWith(key)` happens after appending; also `buffer.length > key.length` slice. Careful ordering: append ch; if endsWith key → colon. Else trim. That works since key starts with `"`.

Hmm, but the field could be matched inside a longer string. Accept.

`inside` getter: phase === 'value'.

`finish()`: return '' (nothing pending meaningful).

Also we want `sawField` to know whether we ever found it — if not found, front-end falls back to showing nothing then the final answer. Provide `get found(): boolean` = phase !== 'seek'.

Edge: unicode escape `\u4e2d` → parse hex 4 chars.

```ts
const decoder = createTextFieldDecoder();
let result: LlmResult | null = null;
let pending = '';
for await (const ev of input.client.stream(request)) {
  if (ev.type === 'delta') {
    pending += decoder.push(ev.text);
    if (pending.length >= DELTA_FLUSH_CHARS) { await emitDelta(probe.id, 'text', pending); pending=''; }
  } else if (ev.type === 'reasoning') {
    await emitDelta(probe.id, 'thinking', ev.text);
  } else if (ev.type === 'done') {
    result = ev.result;
  }
}
pending += decoder.finish();
if (pending.length > 0) await emitDelta(...);
if (result === null) throw new Error('作答没有拿到最终结果');
```

<details><summary>🧠 思考过程</summary>

Now I understand the whole pipeline. The P1 task: add streaming to live mode so the front-end can show token-by-token output and reasoning.

Design: The five stages all use `client.complete()`. To add streaming visible to front-end, we need to:
1. Add a new RunEvent type e.g. `{ type: 'delta', stage, purpose, round, text }` or a `thinking` event.
2. The stages need to call `client.stream()` instead of `complete()` when streaming is desired, and forward deltas.
3. But events go through `onEvent` → appendEvent to DB + SSE. Writing every token delta to DB would be terrible. So deltas should be SSE-only, not persisted.

Hmm, this is a design decision. Let me think about the cleanest approach given the architecture:
- `runSession` emits RunEvents via onEvent; pipeline persists each event and forwards to SSE.
- For streaming, we need a separate channel that's not persisted.

Option: add `onDelta` callback to RunSessionInput, which pipeline forwards only to SSE (not to appendEvent). And SSE event name `delta`.

Then stages need a way to stream. Approach: introduce a helper `completeWithStream(client, request, onDelta)` that if `onDelta` is provided, uses `client.stream(req)` and accumulates text + forwards delta/reasoning; else uses `complete`. That keeps replay/record working and makes behavior identical (the accumulated text equals the recorded text).

Important: for replay mode, `stream()` yields chunks from cassette; fine. For live, `stream()` yields real deltas. For record mode, live.ts stream writes cassette with chunks.

But careful: `client.complete()` in record mode also writes cassette. Both fine.

Also: the accounting wrapper `withAccounting` handles stream — notes at done. Good.

But there's a subtlety: if we use stream in record mode, the cassette gets `chunks`. Fine.

Another subtlety: test scripted client's `stream` yields `{type:'reasoning'}` then one delta then done. That works.

Now the delta events need to reach the browser. Currently SSE writes `event: <event.type>`. If we add a `delta` type to RunEvent, pipeline would appendEvent it to DB — bad. So we need `onDelta` separate from `onEvent`.

Let me design:

In run.ts:
```ts
export type RunStage = 'extract' | 'probe' | 'answer' | 'verdict' | 'suggest';

export interface RunDelta {
  stage: string;
  round: number | null;
  /** 这条增量属于哪一次调用：同一阶段可能并发多条（追问、作答、判定、建议） */
  callId: string;
  kind: 'reasoning' | 'text';
  text: string;
}
```

And `RunSessionInput.onDelta?: (delta: RunDelta) => void`.

The callId: to let the front-end group deltas per call. For probe stage, one call per interviewer. We can use purpose + interviewerId. For answer, probeId. For verdict, claimId. For suggest, claimId. For extract, 'extract'.

Simplest: give each stage a stable label like `probe:tech`, `answer:P1`, `verdict:C1`, `suggest:C2`, `extract`.

Now how do stages get the onDelta? They take `client`; we'd need to pass a per-call delta sink. Cleanest: introduce a small interface in llm/stream-call.ts:

```ts
export interface DeltaSink {
  (event: { kind: 'reasoning' | 'text'; text: string }): void;
}

export async function callModel(
  client: LlmClient,
  request: LlmRequest,
  onDelta?: DeltaSink,
): Promise<LlmResult>
```

If onDelta is undefined → `client.complete(request)` (fast path, no behavior change for tests? No—tests use scripted client whose complete works). Actually if onDelta is undefined we keep complete. If provided, we use stream and accumulate.

But there's a risk: live `stream()` may behave differently from `complete()` in edge cases. Since we always want streaming in live mode for the UX, the pipeline will always pass onDelta when SSE is in use. Then `complete` path is only for tests / non-stream runs (`?stream=0`). Hmm, that means the JSON path and SSE path could take different code paths — that violates "同一条代码路径". Actually it's the same stage code, just different transport inside the LLM layer. Acceptable but should be documented.

Alternative: always stream, and forward deltas only if a sink exists. That keeps one code path always. Risk: `stream()` for replay/record/live must be robust. It is implemented for all three. And scripted test client implements stream. Actually orchestrator tests use `createScriptedClient` which has stream. So always streaming would exercise stream in tests too. But `complete()` is used in extract etc.; changing to always-stream means tests that assert `countPurpose` still fine.

Hmm, but one risk: if a live provider returns non-streaming SSE weirdness. Let's be careful but pragmatic: use stream when a sink exists, else complete. Actually to keep "one code path", better: add option `streaming: boolean` in RunSessionInput, and helper `callModel(client, req, opts)`.

Hmm. Let me think about what's minimal, testable, and honest.

I'll do: `callModel(client, request, sink?)`:
- sink undefined → complete
- sink defined → stream, accumulate text and reasoning, return LlmResult from done event.

And runSession passes a sink built from `input.onDelta` (undefined when no onDelta). Pipeline passes onDelta when streaming (SSE). For `?stream=0` JSON mode, no deltas — uses complete. That's a documented, small difference; both produce identical results because the LLM layer's stream and complete parse the same payload.

Given UX scoring emphasizes "流式、思考可视化", the best is: a live ticker panel that shows, for the current call, the reasoning text (folded) and the text being produced. Since our pipeline is JSON-output for all stages, the streamed text is raw JSON — ugly. Hmm. Streaming raw JSON to the user is not great UX.

Options:
(a) Show raw stream as "模型正在输出的原始 token" in a monospace ticker — honest, and actually shows the mechanism. With JSON it looks technical but it IS the model output.

<details><summary>🧠 思考过程</summary>

Now I need to design the streaming. Let me think about the design carefully.

Goal: live mode should stream tokens to the frontend, showing "思考可视化" (reasoning + text deltas).

Constraints:
1. Concurrency: probe/answer/verdict/suggest use Promise.all across multiple calls. Deltas from multiple calls interleave → need a channel id.
2. Don't break replay determinism.
3. Existing test asserts event sequence `['stage','claims','stage','probes','stage','answers','budget','stage','verdicts','stage','suggestions']` — adding a new event type would break it. Options: (a) update the test, (b) put deltas on a separate channel not in RunEvent.

Design decision: Add a `stream` event to RunEvent? That would break that assertion. But actually the assertion likely filters? Let me check orchestrator-run.test.ts to see. Better: add delta events but make them a separate callback (onDelta) rather than onEvent — keeping the persisted event log clean. But the frontend needs them via SSE. Hmm.

Actually think about what's best product-wise and architecture-wise:
- Persisted events go to SQLite (appendEvent) — we do NOT want to persist thousands of delta chunks. That would bloat the DB and make replay event counts huge. So deltas should NOT be persisted.
- SSE needs to carry them.

So: separate channel. `onEvent` → persisted + SSE. Add `onStream?: (chunk: StreamChunk) => void` for transient deltas → SSE only, not persisted.

That's clean: "流式分片是瞬时观感，不进事件表" — good architecture argument for the tech doc.

But wait — SSE writes: the http app writes each event with `id: seq`. Deltas have no seq. We can write them with `event: 'stream'` and no id, or id as a transient counter. Careful: frontend uses id for resume (from=). Deltas without id are fine — but the SSE `id` field sets Last-Event-ID. Better to not send id for deltas. Let me check how the http app writes.

Also need: frontend state folding must handle 'stream' events. And the frontend needs to display live text somewhere — "等待作答…" placeholder replaced by streaming text.

Let me scope this sensibly. This is a big feature. Let me plan:

**Server side:**
1. `types.ts`: no change needed (LlmStreamEvent already has reasoning/delta/done).
2. New file `orchestrator/stream.ts`? Or add to run.ts: define
```ts
export interface StreamChunk {
  /** 这次模型调用在本次沙盘内的序号，从 1 开始 */
  callId: string;
  stage: string;
  /** 这一片属于哪个目标：主张编号 / 追问编号 / 面试官 */
  targetId: string | null;
  kind: 'reasoning' | 'text';
  text: string;
```

<details><summary>🧠 思考过程</summary>

Now let me design the streaming feature.

Goal: live mode should stream tokens to the frontend so "思考可视化" works.

Design constraints:
- Five call sites use `client.complete()`. Concurrency in probe/answer/verdict/suggest.
- Stream events must carry a channel id so the frontend can attribute deltas.
- `RunEvent` union additions would break `orchestrator-run.test.ts` event sequence assertion. But that assertion only checks `stage`-ish list? Let me check: it asserts `['stage','claims','stage','probes','stage','answers','budget','stage','verdicts','stage','suggestions']`. If I add a new event type like `thinking`/`token`, the emitted events would include them and the assertion would break — I'd need to update the test. That's fine, but note: emitting a token event per delta would flood the event log (appendEvent per delta → DB writes, SSE writes). That's expensive: for 8 claims × ~500 chars each, thousands of events persisted. Bad.

Better design: **streaming deltas are NOT persisted events**. They go through a separate channel: a `onDelta` callback (ephemeral), delivered over SSE as a distinct event name that is NOT stored in the event table. This keeps:
- event log = truth, deterministic, replayable
- token deltas = ephemeral UI sugar, no DB write

So: add to `RunSessionInput` an optional `onStream?: (chunk: StreamChunk) => void` where `StreamChunk = {callId, purpose, channel, kind:'reasoning'|'delta', text}`. And a `RunStreamChunk` type exported from run.ts.

Then in `pipeline.runAndPersist`, `onStream` passed through to a callback `input.onStream?: (chunk) => void` which the HTTP layer writes as SSE event `stream`.

Frontend: `sandboxReducer` handles `{type:'stream'}` events... but `asRunEvent` filters. Better: web's `RunEvent` union adds `{type:'stream'; callId; purpose; kind; text}`. And the reducer keeps a `streams` map keyed by callId, accumulating text, and UI shows the currently-streaming text in the "等待作答…" place, and a "思考中" panel for reasoning.

Now: how to plumb streaming into the orchestrator stages? Each stage needs to call `client.stream(req)` and collect. Simplest approach: add a helper in a new file `packages/server/src/orchestrator/streaming.ts`:

```ts
export async function completeStreamed(
  client: LlmClient,
  req: LlmRequest,
  sink: StreamSink | undefined,
  callId: string,
): Promise<LlmResult>
```
Where it iterates `client.stream(req)`, calls sink({...}) for reasoning/delta, and returns the done result.

But careful: for replay clients this yields chunked deltas — good, replay gets real streaming through the same path. And `withAccounting` stream branch notes once. 

But there's a subtlety: scripted test client's stream yields '（脚本化思考）' reasoning + delta + done — fine.

Another subtlety: `live.ts` stream throws if not configured — same as complete.

Now where do callIds come from? Deterministic: `${purpose}:${index}`. For probe: `probe:${interviewerId}`. For answer: `answer:${probeId}`. For verdict: `verdict:${claimId}`. For suggest: `suggest:${claimId}`. For extract: `extract`. These are stable and human-meaningful, and they let the UI say "技术面试官正在思考" or attach deltas to a specific probe card.

So the sink signature: `onStream?: (chunk: RunStreamChunk) => void` with
```ts
export interface RunStreamChunk {
  /** 哪个调用在吐字，前端用它把分片贴回对应的卡片 */
  callId: string;
  stage: string;       // extract/probe/answer/verdict/suggest
  kind: 'reasoning' | 'delta';
  text: string;
}
```

Frontend keeps `streams: Record<callId, {stage, reasoning, text, done}>`. When the stage event for the next phase arrives or the corresponding result event arrives, we clear/finish. Simpler: keep a map, and on `probes`/`answers`/`verdicts`/`suggestions`/`claims` events, clear streams for that stage.

UI presentation:
1. During running, ClaimBoard's "等待作答…" gets replaced by live text of `answer:<probeId>` if present.
2. A new small "思考流" panel in the stage rail or top showing the most recent reasoning/delta with a typewriter feel — that's the "思考可视化" money shot.

Also important: `RunEvent` type assertion in orchestrator-run.test.ts. Since streams are NOT RunEvents, that test stays green. 

But `http.test.ts` asserts events=16 and `from=7` continuation = 9. If stream chunks are not persisted as events, counts stay. But SSE will emit additional `stream` events which the test's SSE reader may count? Let me check how http.test.ts collects SSE. Need to read it.

Also `smoke.mjs` checks "事件 seq 连续" — streams not persisted, fine.

Let me also consider: should stream chunks be recorded so that a page refresh mid-run can show them? Not necessary — the final text lands in the answers/verdicts events.

Another consideration: throttling. Live stream deltas per token could be thousands of SSE writes. Fine-ish, but let's add a coalescing: buffer deltas and flush at most every N ms? Since onStream is sync and run inside the stream loop, simple approach: accumulate and flush when buffer >= 24 chars or on reasoning boundary. Simpler: emit each delta as-is (live.ts already yields per SSE chunk, not per token — DeepSeek yields chunks of a few chars). Replay yields 24-char chunks. So volume is moderate. Good — no throttling needed. But note SSE writes are per event; for a 500-char answer that's maybe 50 events. Acceptable.

   ```ts
   export async function streamOnce(client, request, channel, onToken?): Promise<LlmResult> {
     let last: LlmResult | null = null;
     let text = '';
     for await (const ev of client.stream(request)) {
       if (ev.type === 'delta') { text += ev.text; onToken?.({channel, kind:'delta', text: ev.text}); }
       else if (ev.type === 'reasoning') { onToken?.({channel, kind:'reasoning', text: ev.text}); }
       else if (ev.type === 'done') last = ev.result;
     }
     if (last === null) throw new LlmError('bad-request', '流式调用没有给出最终结果：' + request.purpose);
     return last;
   }
   ```

Now I need to design the streaming feature. Key design decision: how to route deltas to the frontend without breaking the event sequence assertion.

Let me think about the design:

Option A: Add a new `RunEvent` type `stream` with `{type:'stream', channel, purpose, label, text}` — but this pollutes the event log (persisted) and breaks the existing event-sequence test, plus would bloat the DB with hundreds of delta rows.

Option B: Keep RunEvent as-is (persisted truth), and add a **separate side channel** for streaming: `onDelta` callback on `RunSessionInput` that doesn't go to `onEvent`. Then `runAndPersist` passes an `onDelta` that emits SSE events of type `delta` but does NOT append to the event table. This keeps:
- event table = truth (unchanged, 16 events)
- SSE = event + delta events
- replay determinism unchanged

This is clearly the right design. The deltas are transient UI telemetry, not facts to persist. And the event log stays replay-comparable.

Now, how do stages consume stream? Need a helper in llm layer: `collectStream(client, req, onDelta)` returning `{text, usage, digest}`. Actually simpler: a function `completeStreaming(client, req, onDelta?): Promise<LlmResult>` that:
- if no onDelta → just `client.complete(req)` (preserves old behavior and keeps cassette usage in record mode? No — complete also writes cassettes in live mode. Both work.)
- Actually better: always use stream when onDelta provided; else use complete. Hmm but then record mode with onDelta writes cassettes with chunks; without writes without chunks. That's fine since replay falls back to chunkText.

But careful: tests use scripted client whose `complete` returns the text; `stream` yields reasoning + delta + done. Both work.

Simplest and most consistent: add a helper `runStreaming(client, req, sink)`:

```ts
export async function completeViaStream(
  client: LlmClient,
  req: LlmRequest,
  onDelta?: DeltaSink,
): Promise<LlmResult> {
  if (onDelta === undefined) return client.complete(req);
  let last: LlmResult | null = null;
  for await (const ev of client.stream(req)) {
    if (ev.type === 'delta') onDelta({kind:'delta', text: ev.text});
    else if (ev.type === 'reasoning') onDelta({kind:'reasoning', text: ev.text});
    else last = ev.result;
  }
  if (last === null) throw new LlmError('bad-request', '流式调用没有返回最终结果：' + req.purpose);
  return last;
}
```

Wait — one problem: in live mode, if onDelta is undefined we call complete. But the mode choice shouldn't depend on the sink for correctness. That's fine.

Hmm, but actually there's a subtlety: for the demo (replay mode), do we want streaming deltas? Yes! Replay's chunkText gives nice streaming. Currently replay mode's stream is never used. So enabling streaming everywhere means the demo shows streaming too.

Now I have the full picture. Let me design the streaming feature.

Design goals:
1. live mode should stream tokens to the frontend (逐字吐字 + reasoning).
2. Must not break replay determinism (cassette addressing by digest — unaffected).
3. Concurrency: 5 places use Promise.all. Streaming deltas from concurrent calls would interleave. Need channel identity.

Design: introduce a **streaming sink** abstraction. Instead of changing RunEvent (which would break the event-sequence test and pollute the persisted event log — each token would be a DB row, terrible), I'll add a **separate side channel**:

`RunSessionInput.onDelta?: (delta: LlmDelta) => void | Promise<void>`

where `LlmDelta = { stage: StageName; channel: string; kind: 'delta'|'reasoning'; text: string }`.

Channel id must identify which call. For each stage:
- extract: channel = 'extract'
- probe: channel = 'probe:' + contract.id (interviewer)
- answer: channel = 'answer:' + probe.id
- verdict: channel = 'verdict:' + claim.id
- suggest: channel = 'suggest:' + claim.id

And these are emitted as **SSE events** with a distinct event name, e.g. `event: delta`, `data: {channel, stage, kind, text}`. They should NOT be persisted to the DB event log (otherwise thousands of rows). But then `GET /api/sessions/:id/events` history replay wouldn't have them — that's fine, history view shows final results.

Wait, but the `onEvent` in pipeline both persists AND pushes. I need a separate callback that only pushes to SSE. So `PersistRunInput.onDelta?: (delta) => void | Promise<void>`.

Now, how to wire streaming into the 5 stages. Each stage currently does `await client.complete(request)`. I'll add a helper in a new file `src/orchestrator/stream.ts`:

```ts
export interface DeltaSink { (d: LlmDelta): void | Promise<void> }

export async function completeWithStream(
  client: LlmClient,
  request: LlmRequest,
  opts: { stage: StageName; channel: string; onDelta?: DeltaSink; throttleMs?: number },
): Promise<LlmResult>
```

Implementation: if `onDelta === undefined` → just `client.complete(request)`. Hmm, but then live mode without a sink doesn't stream. Actually if there's no sink we can still use complete() — cheaper. But that means two code paths. Hmm.

Actually there's a subtlety: for replay, `stream()` uses `entry.response.chunks` or chunkText — deterministic. Using stream() everywhere would give the same final result. Using complete() is simpler. Let me make the helper: if no sink, use complete(); if sink, use stream() and forward deltas.

Hmm, but that creates a divergence risk: live+no-sink uses complete (one HTTP request), live+sink uses stream. Both hit the same API. For record mode, stream writes a cassette with chunks, complete writes without. That means **cassette content depends on whether a sink was attached** — the digest is the same, so a stream-recorded cassette would be overwritten by a complete-recorded one and vice versa. Not a correctness problem (both have `text`), but it makes record mode non-deterministic in file content. Acceptable? Better: always use stream() when mode is 'live' or 'record'... no, simpler: **always use stream()** in the five stages. That keeps one code path. Then replay naturally streams too (already works), and scripted test client streams too.

But: always stream() changes the test expectations. http.test.ts pins `outputTokens=120` = 6 calls × 20 — scripted stream yields done with USAGE, so unchanged. `events=16` — RunEvent count unchanged since deltas don't go into RunEvent. Good.

But there's a performance consideration: with stream(), live mode does an SSE HTTP request per call — that's the same request either way, just incremental reading. Fine.

However `withAccounting` wraps stream and notes on done — fine.

### `packages/server/src/llm/stream.ts` (new)

```ts
/** 一次模型调用的直播事件：与业务事件分开走，不落库。 */
export type StreamEvent =
  | { type: 'stream-start'; id: string; purpose: string; label: string; interviewerId: string | null; claimId: string | null; probeId: string | null }
  | { type: 'stream-reasoning'; id: string; text: string }
  | { type: 'stream-text'; id: string; text: string }
  | { type: 'stream-end'; id: string; chars: number; elapsedMs: number; replayed: boolean; error: string | null };

export type StreamSink = (event: StreamEvent) => void | Promise<void>;
```

And a helper:

```ts
export interface StreamCallMeta {
  purpose: string;
  label: string;
  interviewerId?: string;
  claimId?: string;
  probeId?: string;
}

let counter... // no module state — must be per-run
```

Module-level counter would break determinism across runs (ids keep incrementing). Ids should be deterministic per run → pass a factory. Let me make a `createStreamRecorder(sink)` returning `{ call(meta, req, client): Promise<LlmResult> }` with an internal counter.

```ts
export interface StreamCaller {
  (client: LlmClient, meta: StreamCallMeta, req: LlmRequest): Promise<LlmResult>;
}

export function createStreamCaller(sink: StreamSink | undefined): StreamCaller {
  let seq = 0;
  return async function call(client, meta, req) {
    if (sink === undefined) return client.complete(req);   // 没人在听就不直播：走非流式，省一次连接
    seq += 1;
    const id = 'L' + String(seq);
    await sink({ type:'stream-start', id, ... });
    let text = '';
    let reasoning = '';
    let result: LlmResult | null = null;
    try {
      for await (const ev of client.stream(req)) {
        if (ev.type === 'delta') { text += ev.text; await sink({type:'stream-text', id, text: ev.text}); }
        else if (ev.type === 'reasoning') { reasoning += ev.text; await sink({type:'stream-reasoning', id, text: ev.text}); }
        else result = ev.result;
      }
    } catch (err) {
      await sink({ type:'stream-end', id, chars: text.length, elapsedMs: 0, replayed: false, error: msg });
      throw err;
    }
    const done = result ?? { text, reasoning, usage: EMPTY_USAGE, digest:'', replayed:false, elapsedMs:0 };
    await sink({ type:'stream-end', id, chars: done.text.length, elapsedMs: done.elapsedMs, replayed: done.replayed, error: null });
    return done;
  };
}
```

Wait — but when sink is undefined we use `complete`. That means live mode without SSE (e.g. `?stream=0`) uses non-streaming. That's a nice optimization AND keeps existing tests (which use `?stream=0` and expect outputTokens=120) unchanged? Actually with scripted client, complete vs stream both return usage 20. Now the five stages:

1. **extract.ts**: `call({purpose:'extract', label:'读简历，抽主张'}, request)`.
2. **probe.ts**: per interviewer: `call({purpose:'probe', label:'组织追问', interviewerId: contract.id}, request)`.
3. **answer.ts**: `call({purpose:'answer', label:'求职者作答', probeId: probe.id, claimId: probe.claimId}, request)`.
4. **verdict.ts**: `call({purpose:'verdict', label:'复盘判定', claimId: claim.id}, request)`.
5. **suggest.ts**: `call({purpose:'suggest', label:'改写建议', claimId: claim.id}, request)`.

<details><summary>🧠 思考过程</summary>

Now let me design the streaming feature.

Design goals:
1. live/record mode: stream tokens to frontend (thinking + content).
2. replay mode: keep existing behavior — replay client already streams chunkText; but currently orchestrator uses complete() so replay also doesn't stream. If we switch orchestrator to stream(), replay will also stream chunks (nice — the fake streaming becomes real streaming through the pipeline). But that could change event counts in tests (http.test.ts expects events=16). If we emit stream deltas as RunEvent, events table would get extra rows → tests break and event log bloats.

Better design: **streaming deltas do NOT go into the event log (appendEvent)**. They go through a separate callback channel: `onToken` / `onDelta` in RunSessionInput, and pipeline passes it to a separate SSE-only channel (not persisted). This keeps: replay determinism, event log unchanged, tests stable.

So:
- Add `RunStreamEvent` type in run.ts? Better a separate type: `{ callId: string; purpose: string; kind: 'reasoning'|'delta'; text: string }`.
- Add a new module `packages/server/src/orchestrator/streaming.ts` with a helper `completeWithStream(client, request, sink)` that: if sink is undefined → use `client.complete(req)` (backwards compatible, no behavior change); else iterate `client.stream(req)` collecting text + emitting deltas to sink, and returns LlmResult.

Wait — but if we always use stream() when a sink exists, replay mode also streams chunks. That's fine and desirable (front shows typing). But careful: replay's `stream()` yields chunks; `done` gives result. Fine.

But there's a subtlety: `client.stream()` in live mode makes a streaming HTTP request. If the sink is not provided (e.g. `?stream=0` JSON mode), we use complete(). Good — that keeps non-streaming path identical (and cheaper for tests).

Actually better: default should be to use stream when available? No — keep `complete` for `?stream=0` to avoid changing behavior in tests that count `llm calls`... wait, `withAccounting` notes once per call either way, so call count unchanged. But `http.test.ts` uses `app.request` with `?stream=0`? Let me check: it asserts llm calls=6 and events=16. If we make stream deltas not persisted, events stay 16. llm calls stay 6. outputTokens 120 stays (scripted usage same). Good.

But one risk: the scripted client's `stream()` yields delta events; if tests use `?stream=0` we call complete() → fine.

Hmm, but I want to test that streaming actually happens. I'll add a new test file for the streaming channel.

Now: how to wire the delta channel to SSE without persisting?

- `RunSessionInput` gets `onDelta?: (event: TokenEvent) => void | Promise<void>`.
- `runAndPersist` PersistRunInput gets `onDelta?: (event: TokenEvent) => void | Promise<void>` — passed straight through to runSession, NOT to appendEvent.
- http app: in the SSE branch, provide `onDelta: async (e) => { await stream.writeSSE({ event: 'token', data: JSON.stringify(e) }) }`. Note: no `id` — because these aren't in the event log; if we give them ids, EventSource resumption would be confused. Good, keep id absent.

Wait — but ordering/interleaving: multiple concurrent calls. The `callId` disambiguates. Frontend can show "正在追问" per interviewer. Simplest useful UI: a single "live typing" panel showing the latest in-flight call's text, tagged with purpose + callId. But better UX: attach deltas to the interviewer/probe. That requires mapping callId → which interviewer. We can make callId deterministic and meaningful: `purpose + '#' + index` e.g. `probe#tech` (interviewerId known), `answer#P3` (probeId known), `verdict#C2` (claimId), `suggest#C2`, `extract#1`. That's much better — frontend can attach to claim/probe.

So define:
```ts
export interface TokenEvent {
  /** 调用的稳定标识：阶段名 + 目标（如 probe#tech、answer#P3、verdict#C2） */
  callId: string;
  purpose: string;
  /** reasoning = 模型的思考过程，delta = 正文增量 */
  kind: 'reasoning' | 'delta';
  text: string;
}
```
And `done` signal? Frontend needs to know when a call finished to clear "typing". Add `kind: 'reasoning'|'delta'|'end'`. For 'end', text=''. Hmm, simpler: add a separate event `{... kind:'done'}`. Let's include `'done'` in the union: `kind: 'reasoning' | 'delta' | 'done'`. When done, frontend clears the typing buffer for that callId (and could keep the final text). Actually the final authoritative content arrives via the regular stage events (probes/answers/verdicts/suggestions), so on done we just drop the buffer.

That's clean: token events are purely ephemeral; nothing about correctness depends on them.

Now the helper. In `streaming.ts`:

```ts
export interface StreamSink { (event: TokenEvent): void | Promise<void>; }

export async function completeWithStream(
  client: LlmClient,
  request: LlmRequest,
  callId: string,
  sink?: StreamSink,
): Promise<LlmResult>
```
Implementation: if sink undefined → return client.complete(request).
Else:
```ts
let text = '';
let reasoning = '';
let result: LlmResult | null = null;
for await (const ev of client.stream(request)) {
  if (ev.type === 'reasoning') { reasoning += ev.text; await sink({callId, purpose: request.purpose, kind:'reasoning', text: ev.text}); }
  else if (ev.type === 'delta') { text += ev.text; await sink({...kind:'delta'...}); }
  else { result = ev.result; }
}
if (result === null) throw new LlmError('http-error', ...)  // 流断了却没有 done
await sink({callId, purpose, kind:'done', text: ''});
return result;
```
Hmm, if stream throws mid-way (e.g. cassette-missing in replay), the error propagates — same as complete(). Good. But should we emit a done/end on error? No; the stage-level error event covers it.

Wait, one issue: for replay, `client.stream()` loads the cassette and yields; but `complete()` also loads. Same result. Good.

Another: `reasoning` accumulates but `LlmResult.reasoning` already has it from live. We ignore.

Potential problem: some clients may not support stream? All three do. But `withAccounting` wraps stream — fine.

No — complete doesn't stream.

Alternative cleaner approach: **make the sink part of the client wrapper**: `createStreamingClient(inner, sink)` whose `complete(req)` internally iterates `inner.stream(req)` and emits tokens, and whose `stream(req)` also emits tokens then yields. Better: pass identity via `LlmRequest`? Adding an optional field to LlmRequest would change the digest (digestFor uses shapeOf which picks specific fields, so adding a field to the type but not to shapeOf keeps digest stable). But adding `callId` to LlmRequest means the cassette doesn't record it — fine.

But the stage modules receive `input.client` and call `complete` on it. Alternative: since `probe.ts` already builds `request` with `purpose: 'probe'` and knows contract.id, I'll add to each stage input an optional `onDelta?: DeltaSink` and a helper in streaming.ts:

```ts

```

Then:

- suggest.ts: `streamed(input.client, request, 'suggest#' + claim.id, input.onDelta)`

<details><summary>🧠 思考过程</summary>

Now I have the shape. Let me design the streaming approach.

Design: Add a `stream` sink option to `LlmClient` calls? The simplest robust approach that doesn't break concurrency and doesn't pollute RunEvent:

Add a new optional parameter to each stage input: `onStream?: StreamSink` where `StreamSink = (event: StreamTick) => void`, and `StreamTick = { channel: string; kind: 'reasoning'|'delta'; text: string }`. The channel identifies the call (e.g. `probe:tech:r1`, `answer:P1`, `verdict:C1`, `suggest:C1`, `extract`).

Then run.ts emits a new RunEvent type: `{ type: 'stream'; channel: string; kind: 'reasoning'|'delta'; text: string }`. But the http.test asserts event counts (16 events for panel=tech), and orchestrator-run.test asserts the exact event type sequence. Both would break.

Options:
- Keep stream deltas out of RunEvent/appendEvent, and instead have pipeline pass a separate `onStream` callback through to runSession, which the HTTP layer writes to SSE as a different SSE event name (e.g. `event: stream`). Then RunEvent type sequence unchanged, appendEvent unchanged, event count unchanged. That satisfies "don't break tests". SSE gets extra `event: stream` frames. Smoke only checks `event: stage` exists and ends — fine.

But wait: runAndPersist's onEvent gives seq. Stream frames would have no seq — that's fine, they're ephemeral. Frontend `readSseStream` parses all events; `sandboxReducer` 'sse' branch: name='stream' → `asRunEvent('stream', data)` returns null (unknown name) → falls to default → returns base. Need to add handling in web state to accumulate stream text per channel, and render it.

Hmm, but this is a fair amount of work. Let's scope carefully:

1. Server: 
   - types: add `StreamTick` type? Put in a new file `src/llm/stream.ts` or in types.ts. Add `StreamSink = (tick: StreamTick) => void`.
   - New helper `streamOnce(client, req, sink?): Promise<LlmResult>` — if sink undefined, use `complete()` (keeps replay fast and tests unchanged); else consume `client.stream(req)` and forward ticks, return done result. Put it in `src/llm/stream.ts`? Actually orchestrator-level: `src/orchestrator/streaming.ts`.
   - Each stage: replace `await input.client.complete(request)` with `await callModel(input.client, request, channel, input.onStream)`.
   - Channels: extract → `extract`; probe → `probe:<interviewerId>:r<round>`; answer → `answer:<probeId>`; verdict → `verdict:<claimId>`; suggest → `suggest:<claimId>`.

```ts
import type { LlmClient, LlmRequest, LlmResult, LlmStreamEvent } from '../llm/types.ts';

export interface StreamChannel {
  /** 频道标识：同一场质询里唯一，前端按它把增量拼成一段文本 */
  id: string;
  /** 阶段名，与 RunEvent 的 stage 对齐 */
  stage: string;
  /** 给人看的名字，例如「技术面试官」 */
  label: string;
}

export interface StreamDelta {
  channel: StreamChannel;
  kind: 'reasoning' | 'text';
  text: string;
}

export type StreamListener = (delta: StreamDelta) => void | Promise<void>;

export interface CollectOptions {
  client: LlmClient;
  request: LlmRequest;
  channel: StreamChannel;
  onDelta?: StreamListener;
}

export async function collectStream(opts: CollectOptions): Promise<LlmResult> {
  let last: LlmResult | null = null;
  for await (const event of opts.client.stream(opts.request)) {
    if (event.type === 'done') { last = event.result; continue; }
    if (opts.onDelta) await opts.onDelta({ channel: opts.channel, kind: event.type, text: event.text });
  }
  if (last === null) throw new Error('模型流没有给出结束事件：' + opts.request.purpose);
  return last;
}
```

Design:
- Add a new event type `stream` to RunEvent: `{ type: 'stream'; stage: string; callId: string; kind: 'delta'|'reasoning'; text: string }`? But run.ts emits events that go into DB via appendEvent. Storing every delta as an event row would bloat the DB massively (thousands of rows).

Better: keep streaming out of the persisted event log. Add a separate callback `onStream` on RunSessionInput / PersistRunInput that is NOT persisted, and http/app.ts writes those as SSE events with a distinct event name (e.g. `event: token`), no seq id (or a synthetic id). But SSE `id:` field is used for resume; if we omit id, browser keeps last id — fine. Actually writeSSE without id is fine.

But careful: the events endpoint replays history; streamed deltas won't be in history — that's correct and honest (like a transcript). Frontend will just show streaming text live.

So plan:
1. `packages/server/src/llm/types.ts` — already has reasoning/delta/done. Add helper `collectStream`? Better create a shared helper in orchestrator: `completeViaStream(client, req, sink?)` that consumes client.stream() and returns LlmResult. Put in `packages/server/src/llm/collect.ts`.

Actually cleanest: add `src/llm/collect.ts`:
```ts
export interface StreamSink { (event: {kind:'delta'|'reasoning'; text:string}): void }
export async function collectStream(client, req, sink?): Promise<LlmResult>
```
It iterates client.stream(req); for delta/reasoning call sink; capture done result. If no done → throw LlmError('bad-request', ...)? Use a plain Error.

Where do callIds come from? Each stage builds requests. Simplest: caller passes a label. In probe.ts: `'probe:' + contract.id`. In answer.ts: `'answer:' + probe.id`. In verdict.ts: `'verdict:' + claim.id`. In suggest.ts: `'suggest:' + claim.id`. In extract.ts: `'extract'`.

But we can just fire-and-forget with a promise chain? Risky ordering. Let's make it `void | Promise<void>` and await it — SSE write per chunk await is fine, adds latency but OK. Actually for live mode, tokens arrive at ~50/s; awaiting a write each is fine.

Goal: live mode should stream deltas to the frontend. Design:

Add a new event type `stream` to RunEvent? That would break the `orchestrator-run.test.ts` event sequence assertion. But we can make streaming deltas NOT go through RunEvent/onEvent (which persists to DB), instead go through a separate callback channel that's only for SSE — not persisted to the DB. That's a good design: thinking/deltas are ephemeral telemetry, not the authoritative event log. And it keeps DB from bloating.

Design:
1. `types.ts` already has `LlmStreamEvent` with reasoning/delta/done.
2. Add to `LlmRequest`? No — instead add an optional `onDelta` hook into the orchestrator input, keyed by call id.

Better: introduce a `StreamSink` type in a new file `packages/server/src/orchestrator/stream.ts`:
```ts
export interface LlmChunk { callId: string; purpose: string; channel: 'reasoning' | 'text'; text: string }
export type LlmDeltaSink = (chunk: LlmChunk) => void | Promise<void>;
```

And a helper `collectStream(client, req, sink, callId): Promise<LlmResult>` that iterates `client.stream(req)`, forwards reasoning/delta to sink, returns the done result. If the client's stream throws... it's the same client, so fine.

Wait — but there's a subtlety: switching from `complete()` to `stream()` changes the request that gets fingerprinted? No — digestFor only hashes system/messages/temperature/maxTokens/json, not the stream flag. And live.ts stream writes cassettes with the same digest. Replay's stream reads the same cassette. Good.

But: does replay client's `complete` still work? Yes but we won't use it.

Another subtlety: replay's stream yields reasoning chunks then text chunks — good for demo.

Now, where do call ids come from? Each stage needs deterministic ids: e.g. `extract-1`, `probe-tech-r1`, `answer-P1`, `verdict-C1`, `suggest-C1`. These are deterministic and replay-friendly.

3. Pipeline: pass an `onChunk` callback through `PersistRunInput` and `RunSessionInput`. HTTP layer: in SSE branch, `onChunk` writes SSE events with `event: 'chunk'` (not persisted). In JSON branch, ignore.

Important: SSE `id:` field — currently uses seq for events. Chunks should NOT set id, otherwise they'd break `Last-Event-ID` resume. Use no id for chunks.

4. Frontend: `state.ts` handle `chunk` event → accumulate into a `streaming` map keyed by callId, for display. Where to display? ClaimBoard's "等待作答…" for answers; StageRail for stage-level thinking.

<details><summary>🧠 思考过程</summary>

Now I have a clear picture. Let me design the live streaming feature.

Design decision: add a new event type `token` (or `stream`) that carries incremental text, but do NOT put it into RunEvent (which is persisted to DB — that would bloat the event log). Better: add a separate optional callback `onToken` on RunSessionInput and PersistRunInput, and the HTTP layer sends SSE events named `token` without writing them to the DB.

Wait, but the test orchestrator-run.test.ts asserts exact event sequence via onEvent — if tokens go through onToken, that test stays green. Good.

So design:
1. `LlmStreamEvent` already has `reasoning` | `delta` | `done`.
2. Add to `types.ts` maybe a helper `collectStream(client, req, onDelta)` — a utility that consumes `client.stream()` and returns LlmResult, invoking a callback for deltas/reasoning. This gives one place to switch complete→stream for all five stages.
3. Each stage's call site uses `await runStream(input.client, request, input.onToken)` instead of `client.complete(request)`.
4. `RunSessionInput` gains `onToken?: (token: StreamToken) => void`; `StreamToken = { channel: string; kind: 'delta'|'reasoning'; text: string }` where channel identifies the call: e.g. `extract`, `probe:tech:r1`, `answer:P1`, `verdict:C1`, `suggest:C2`.
5. Concurrency: channels distinguish. Since the Promise.all is concurrent, we need each call's channel assigned before start. For probe: channel = `probe:<interviewerId>` with round. For answer: `answer:<probeId>`. For verdict: `verdict:<claimId>`. For suggest: `suggest:<claimId>`.
6. Pipeline: `PersistRunInput` gains `onToken`; pass through. `runAndPersist` does NOT appendEvent for tokens. HTTP layer: SSE write with `event: 'token'`, data JSON `{channel, kind, text}` — but no `id` (so no seq). Actually SSE id used for resume. Tokens aren't persisted, so no id.

Hmm, but a simpler approach: keep tokens out of the event log but also throttle. Streaming deltas for every token could flood SSE. Replay chunks are 24 chars; live deltas can be 1-3 chars. That's a lot of SSE writes. Add a coalescing buffer in the HTTP layer? Better: coalesce in the streaming helper — accumulate deltas and flush at most every ~60ms or every ~40 chars. Actually simpler and deterministic: the helper just forwards deltas as they come, and the HTTP SSE layer is fine with many writes (SSE is cheap). But 3000 tokens → 3000 SSE events. That's fine-ish but the front-end reducer would run 3000 times. With React 19 batching it's OK. But pace/timer queue in App would choke? The queue is array shift per tick at pace=0 recursion — 3000 sync dispatches. Acceptable but let's coalesce.

Decision: coalesce in the pipeline/HTTP boundary with a small buffer flushed on a timer? Timers introduce nondeterminism. Better: coalesce by length — flush when buffer ≥ 24 chars or on 'done'. That's deterministic, and replay chunks are already 24 so no change for replay. Live deltas of 1-2 chars get merged into 24-char pieces. 

Where to coalesce: in the stream helper (`llm/stream.ts` → `consumeStream`). It yields `onToken` callbacks with coalesced text. Good — one place, testable.

7. Frontend: 
   - `api.ts`: add `TokenEvent` type `{channel, kind, text}`.
   - `state.ts`: add `streaming: Record<string, string>` to SandboxState? Better: `liveText: { channel: string; text: string }[]`? We want to display "thinking"/"typing" text under the right element. Simplest useful: keep a map `streamText: Record<string, {delta: string; reasoning: string}>`, and clear on the corresponding terminal event? But the reducer is pure and events are replayed in history mode too. Tokens are not persisted, so history won't have them. That's fine — state after 'done' can keep the text but ClaimBoard only shows it while answer is pending ("等待作答…" → show streaming text).
   
   Practical minimal approach: reducer handles `sse` with name 'token': append text to `state.stream[channel]`. Then in ClaimBoard, when a probe has no answer yet, show `stream['answer:'+probe.id]` if present, else 「等待作答…」. And in StageRail, show the active stage's current streaming snippet.
   
   Also on 'result'/'fatal' → clear stream map? Keep it, harmless. Actually after answers event arrives the answer replaces it.

```ts
import type { LlmClient, LlmRequest, LlmResult } from '../llm/types.ts';

export type StreamPhase = 'extract' | 'probe' | 'answer' | 'verdict' | 'suggest';

export interface StageStreamEvent {
  /** 哪一路调用的增量：extract / probe:<面试官> / answer:<追问> / verdict:<主张> / suggest:<主张> */
  channel: string;
  type: 'delta' | 'reasoning';
  text: string;
}

export type StreamSink = (event: StageStreamEvent) => void | Promise<void>;

export function channelOf(stage: StreamPhase, id?: string): string {
  return id === undefined || id === '' ? stage : stage + ':' + id;
}

export async function callStage(
  client: LlmClient,
  request: LlmRequest,
  channel: string,
  onStream?: StreamSink,
): Promise<LlmResult> {
  if (onStream === undefined) return client.complete(request);
  let done: LlmResult | null = null;
  for await (const event of client.stream(request)) {
    if (event.type === 'done') { done = event.result; continue; }
    await onStream({ channel, type: event.type, text: event.text });
  }
  if (done === null) throw new Error('模型流没有给出最终结果：' + channel);
  return done;
}
```

Then each stage: add `onStream?: StreamSink` to input; replace `input.client.complete(request)` with `callStage(input.client, request, channelOf('probe', contract.id), input.onStream)`.

Now I have a full picture. Let me design the streaming feature.

Design goals:
1. In live mode, the five orchestrator stages should use `client.stream()` and emit thinking/delta events to the frontend.
2. Must not break replay reproducibility: cassette addressing is by request digest, unaffected.
3. Must not break the `RunEvent` sequence assertions in orchestrator-run.test.ts — the tests assert `['stage','claims','stage','probes',...]`. So if I add new event types into RunEvent, that assertion breaks. Options:
   a. Add streaming events to a separate channel: `onStream?: (event: StreamEvent) => void` on RunSessionInput, not part of RunEvent. Then the event log (DB) doesn't store them, and SSE emits them as separate SSE event names.
   b. Include them in RunEvent but update tests.

Consideration: for replay of history (`stateFromHistory`), streaming deltas aren't needed — the final text is in the answers/claims events. So streaming deltas are ephemeral, live-only UI sugar. That argues strongly for a separate channel: don't persist them (would bloat the event table: 29 calls × ~50 chunks).

But wait — one important consideration: 比赛 UX 评分点「思考可视化」. If deltas aren't persisted, then replaying history shows no streaming. That's fine — history shows final content.

Hmm, but there's a subtlety: in replay mode, the client's stream() also yields chunks (chunkText). So even in replay mode, if we use stream(), the frontend gets "fake streaming" which looks good for the demo video. And the deltas are ephemeral (not persisted), so replay determinism is unaffected.

So the design:
- New type `StreamChunk` in orchestrator: `{ type: 'thinking' | 'text', channel: string, callId: string, purpose: string, text: string }`.

Actually let me think about what the frontend needs. During a stage, multiple concurrent calls happen. The UI currently shows "等待作答…" for answers. To visualize thinking, we want to show, per interviewer / per probe, the streaming text as it arrives.

Simplification that keeps it honest and simple: emit stream events with a `channel` identifying which logical unit is streaming:

Now, how do we pass this into the five stages? Each stage function takes `client`. We need to thread the stream callback down. Options:

Alternative: add `onStream` + `channel` to each stage input and do the streaming inline in each stage. That's more explicit and gives exact channels. It's 5 call sites to change. Let's look at how each call site can do it:

So a helper in a new file `packages/server/src/orchestrator/streaming.ts`:

```ts
export interface StreamChunk {
  kind: 'reasoning' | 'content';
  text: string;
}

export type StreamSink = (chunk: StreamChunk) => void | Promise<void>;

/**
 * 用 stream() 取一次完整结果，同时把增量喂给 sink。
 */
export async function streamResult(client: LlmClient, request: LlmRequest, sink?: StreamSink): Promise<LlmResult> {
  if (sink === undefined) return client.complete(request);
  let last: LlmResult | null = null;
  for await (const event of client.stream(request)) {
    if (event.type === 'reasoning') await sink({ kind: 'reasoning', text: event.text });
    else if (event.type === 'delta') await sink({ kind: 'content', text: event.text });
    else last = event.result;
  }
  if (last === null) throw new Error('流式调用没有给出最终结果：' + request.purpose);
  return last;
}
```

### File 1: packages/server/src/orchestrator/streaming.ts

```ts
/**
 * 把「一次模型调用」从「等到最后拿一段文本」改成「边收边推」。
 *
 * 为什么要有这一层：赛题 UX 评分项点名要「思考可视化」，而
 * 前面五个阶段原先都走 client.complete()，界面上只能看到阶段名在跳，
 * 模型真正吐出来的字一个都看不见。改走 stream() 之后，
 * 每个阶段都能把增量交给上层，再由 SSE 推给浏览器。
 *
 * 三条纪律：
 * 1. 增量只是**过程**，不落库、不进事件表：事件表存的是可复算的结果，
 *    而把上千条分片写进 SQLite 只会让回放变慢、让历史翻看变吵。
 * 2. 没有 sink 时退回 complete()：JSON 接口、单元测试、批处理都不需要流式，
 *    走同一条代码路径反而要多绕一层。
 * 3. 最终结果一律以 done 事件里的 result 为准，绝不把拼接的增量当结果——
 *    分片可能被中间件重排、也可能丢尾包，只有 done 是权威的。
 */
```

Define in a new file `packages/server/src/orchestrator/streaming.ts`:

```ts
export interface StreamChunk {
  channel: string;   // 形如 probe:tech / answer:P1
  stage: StageName;
  kind: 'reasoning' | 'text';
  text: string;
}
export type StreamSink = (chunk: StreamChunk) => void;

export async function callModel(
  client: LlmClient,
  request: LlmRequest,
  init: { channel: string; stage: StageName; onStream?: StreamSink },
): Promise<LlmResult> {
  const sink = init.onStream;
  if (sink === undefined) return client.complete(request);
  let last: LlmResult | null = null;
  for await (const event of client.stream(request)) {
    if (event.type === 'reasoning') sink({...});
    else if (event.type === 'delta') sink({...});
    else last = event.result;
  }
  if (last === null) throw new Error(...);
  return last;
}
```

Key design decision: how to add streaming without breaking the RunEvent sequence assertion at orchestrator-run.test.ts:157.

Options:
1. Add a new RunEvent type `{ type: 'stream'; ... }` — would break the assertion, and would pollute the event log (persisted events). Not good: thousands of rows in SQLite, and history replay would need to handle.
2. Add a **separate callback** on RunSessionInput: `onStream?: (chunk: StreamChunk) => void | Promise<void>`, not part of RunEvent. Then pipeline forwards it as SSE `event: stream` without persisting to the event table. This keeps the event log clean, keeps orchestrator-run test green, and history replay unaffected.

Option 2 is clearly better. Let's design:

```ts
export interface StreamChunk {
  /** 阶段名，用于前端把分片丢到正确的面板 */
  stage: StageName;
  /** 这一分片属于谁：extract 无；probe=面试官角色；answer=追问 id；verdict/suggest=主张 id */
  target: string;
  kind: 'reasoning' | 'text';
  text: string;
  /** 同一 target 内的序号，从 0 递增，前端可用来丢弃乱序/重复 */
  index: number;
}
```

Hmm, do we need index? For ordering within a stream. Since SSE is ordered and each target is sequential within one generator, index is not strictly needed. But concurrent streams interleave across targets — frontend keeps per-target buffers, so fine.

Simpler:
```ts
export type StreamChunk = {
  stage: StageName;
  /** 这条流分片属于哪个对象：extract 阶段是 ''；probe 是面试官角色；answer 是追问编号；verdict/suggest 是主张编号 */
  target: string;
  kind: 'reasoning' | 'text';
  text: string;
};
```

Now how do stages call stream()? Currently each stage calls `client.complete(request)`. We need to switch to stream and collect. Best: add a helper function in a new module, e.g. `packages/server/src/orchestrator/streaming.ts`:

```ts
export interface StreamSink {
  (chunk: StreamChunk): void | Promise<void>;
}

export interface StreamCollectOptions {
  client: LlmClient;
  request: LlmRequest;
  stage: StageName;
  target: string;
  onStream?: StreamSink;
}

export async function completeStreaming(opts): Promise<LlmResult>
```

Wait — but there's an important subtlety: **replay** mode. In replay, calling `stream()` vs `complete()` reads the same cassette, so no behavior change. In **record** mode, live.ts's stream writes a cassette with chunks — good, and complete writes one without chunks. If we switch everything to stream, then record mode will produce cassettes with chunks (better). Existing 29 scripted-demo cassettes were written by `scripted-demo.ts`'s produce() — need to check whether they contain chunks. If `produce` writes `chunks` maybe not. But replay falls back to chunkText when chunks missing — fine.

Another subtlety: **the digest/cassette addressing**. `digestFor` uses system/messages/temperature/maxTokens/json — not affected by complete vs stream. Good.

BUT: **live.ts's stream implementation** — does it produce the same `text` as complete? Presumably yes.

Another critical subtlety: **withAccounting's stream branch** — currently collects done result and notes the call. Fine.

However: if a stage calls `client.stream()`, then `withAccounting` wrapper's stream is used, which notes once at done. Good.

But careful: `withAccounting` wraps the client in `runAndPersist` before passing to `runSession`. And the stage functions receive `input.client` which is the wrapped client. So switching stage calls to stream() automatically gets accounting. 

Now, should streaming be **always on** or only when a sink is provided? To avoid changing behavior in tests/JSON mode, do: if `onStream` is undefined, use `client.complete()`; else use `client.stream()` and forward. Hmm, but that means the code path differs between SSE and JSON. Actually that's fine and even desirable: the JSON path (`?stream=0`) doesn't need incremental output. But there's a subtle asymmetry risk: the two paths could diverge (a bug seen only in one). Mitigation: the streaming helper always goes through stream() when a sink exists; and SSE is the default path, so streaming path is the well-tested one. Also smoke test uses SSE. OK.

We must test it live with the DeepSeek key. Good — that's exactly what the user asked (test every function). Actually the key was given in-session; I can run live test again.

### 1. `packages/server/src/orchestrator/streaming.ts` (new)
```ts
import type { LlmClient, LlmRequest, LlmResult } from '../llm/types.ts';
import type { StageName } from './prompts.ts';

export interface StreamChunk {
  stage: StageName;
  target: string;
  kind: 'reasoning' | 'text';
  text: string;
}

export type StreamSink = (chunk: StreamChunk) => void | Promise<void>;

export async function completeWithStream(
  client: LlmClient,
  request: LlmRequest,
  meta: { stage: StageName; target: string },
  sink?: StreamSink,
): Promise<LlmResult> {
  if (sink === undefined) return client.complete(request);
  let done: LlmResult | null = null;
  for await (const event of client.stream(request)) {
    if (event.type === 'done') { done = event.result; continue; }
    const kind = event.type === 'reasoning' ? 'reasoning' : 'text';
    await sink({ stage: meta.stage, target: meta.target, kind, text: event.text });
  }
  if (done === null) throw new Error('流式调用没有收到 done 事件：' + request.purpose);
  return done;
}
```

<details><summary>🧠 思考过程</summary>

Now I have a good picture. Let me design the streaming implementation.

Design decision: How to plumb streaming into the orchestrator without breaking existing event sequence tests.

Option A: Add a new RunEvent type `{ type: 'stream', ... }` — breaks the event sequence test at orchestrator-run.test.ts:157 and http.test.ts events count = 16. Bad: also SS stream chunks would be persisted to DB as events → bloats DB.

Option B: Keep RunEvent unchanged; add a **separate channel** — a `onChunk` callback on RunSessionInput that is NOT persisted. Pipeline forwards it to HTTP layer via a distinct SSE event name `stream`. This keeps the event log clean (16 events), keeps the event-sequence test intact, and keeps replay determinism (streaming is a transport detail, not part of the recorded timeline).

I like Option B. But there's an important subtlety: switching the orchestrator from `complete()` to `stream()` changes live behavior. In replay mode, does stream work? Yes — replay client's stream reads from cassette and chunks with chunkText. And live.ts stream works. Record mode stream writes cassette with chunks.

But a risk: if I switch all five stages to stream(), then in **record** mode the cassettes are written by `stream()` — which writes the same entry format (just with chunks). Fine. In replay, existing cassettes (recorded via complete) have no `chunks`, so `chunkText` fallback applies. Fine — the 29 committed demo cassettes were written by scripted-demo's `produce()`, which... let me check: scripted-demo's `produce(req)` writes a cassette — with response containing chunks? The summary says `createDemoScriptedClient`'s stream uses chunkText to yield pieces, and produce() is shared. So the cassette `response` might not have `chunks`. Replay falls back to chunkText. Good.

However — careful: **accounting**. `withAccounting` wraps the client; its stream branch awaits inner.stream and notes at done. Good.

Another important consideration: **the orchestrator's stages return usage from result**. With stream, the done event's result carries usage. Good.

Now, a key design question: how do stages call stream when streaming is enabled but still work when the callback is absent? Two choices:
1. Always use stream() in orchestrator (simplest, uniform). Then in replay mode even `?stream=0` JSON path streams internally — harmless (results identical). Tests using scripted client's stream get `（脚本化思考）` reasoning + one delta. The output text is identical to complete. Usage identical. So the event log stays 16 and behavior identical.

   Risk: scripted client's stream yields text as one delta; fine.
   
   But wait: some tests use scripted clients whose `stream` might not be defined? All clients (scripted, live, replay) implement stream. OK.

   Actually, one risk: `createLiveClient` in **live** mode with streaming — extra complexity of SSE parsing; already tested by llm-live.test.ts. But if a provider doesn't support `stream_options`, hmm. It's already implemented and tested.

2. Only use stream when a chunk callback is provided (i.e., when the transport can consume it). This preserves complete() for the JSON path and for tests. But then live streaming only happens on the SSE path — which is exactly where it matters. And `?stream=0` stays on complete().

   Downside: two code paths → the streaming path is exercised only in SSE tests. But that's manageable: I'll add tests.

I prefer option 2 with a clean abstraction: a helper `runStage(client, request, onChunk)` that, when `onChunk` is undefined, calls `client.complete(request)`; otherwise drains `client.stream(request)` forwarding deltas and returning the done result. This helper lives in a new module `packages/server/src/orchestrator/call.ts`.

Hmm, but there's a subtlety: if we stream, do we still get `reasoning`? Yes, forward as kind 'reasoning'.

```ts
  channel: string;
  /** 阶段，便于前端显示 */
  stage: StageName;
  /** reasoning = 思考过程；text = 正式输出（此时是 JSON） */
  kind: 'reasoning' | 'text';
  /** 增量文本 */
  text: string;
}
```

And `onChunk?: (chunk: StreamChunk) => void`.

Where does channel come from? Each stage knows its identity. I'll add an optional `channel` param to the call helper: `callModel(client, request, { stage, channel, onChunk })`.

Now the stages need to accept `onChunk` in their input interfaces and pass channel. Let me enumerate:

- extract.ts: channel `'extract'`, stage 'extract'
- probe.ts: per interviewer: channel `'probe:' + contract.id`, stage 'probe'. Also could include the round.

```ts
/**
 * 一次模型调用的统一入口。
 *
 * 为什么不直接在五个阶段里调 client.complete()：
 * 赛题 UX 维度点名要「思考可视化」，而十个阶段的输出原先要等整段生成完才能显示。
 * 这里把「怎么调」收成一个函数：没有流式消费者时走 complete（省一次往返解析），
 * 有消费者时走 stream，把增量原样转出去，最后仍然以 done 里的 result 为准。
 *
 * 重要：增量**不是**结果。所有下游逻辑（zod 校验、锚定、记账）用的都是 result.text，
 * 增量只用于界面直播，因此它不落库、不进事件表、不影响回放。
 */
```

Signature:
```ts
export interface StageCall {
  stage: StageName;
  channel: string;
}

export type ChunkSink = (chunk: StreamChunk) => void | Promise<void>;

export async function callModel(
  client: LlmClient,
  request: LlmRequest,
  call: StageCall,
  onChunk?: ChunkSink,
): Promise<LlmResult> {
  if (onChunk === undefined) return client.complete(request);
  let done: LlmResult | null = null;
  for await (const event of client.stream(request)) {
    if (event.type === 'done') { done = event.result; continue; }
    const kind = event.type === 'reasoning' ? 'reasoning' : 'text';
    await onChunk({ channel: call.channel, stage: call.stage, kind, text: event.text });
  }
  if (done === null) throw new LlmError('bad-request', '流式调用没有给出最终结果：' + request.purpose);
  return done;
}
```

So `applyChunk`:
```ts
function applyChunk(state, chunk) {
  const prev = state.live.find(c => c.channel === chunk.channel);
  const field = chunk.kind === 'reasoning' ? 'reasoning' : 'text';
  const merged = tail((prev?.[field] ?? '') + chunk.text, LIVE_MAX_CHARS);
  const next: LiveChannel = prev ? { ...prev, [field]: merged } : { channel, stage, reasoning: '', text: '', [field]: merged };
  const rest = state.live.filter(c => c.channel !== chunk.channel);
  return { ...state, live: [...rest, next] };
}
```
Note this doesn't bump eventCount (chunks aren't events). Good.

```ts
if (name === 'stream') {
  const chunk = asStreamChunk(data);
  return chunk === null ? state : applyChunk(state, chunk);
}
```

Now let me design the streaming implementation.

Design:
1. Add a helper in orchestrator: a function that calls `client.stream(req)` and collects text incrementally, forwarding deltas to a sink. Since all five stages call `client.complete(request)`, I can create a wrapper client that turns `complete` into `stream` internally with a channel label.

Best approach given "don't break replay determinism": Add a new optional field on `RunSessionInput`: `onStream?: (chunk: StreamChunk) => void`. But the stages call `client.complete(...)` directly. So the wrapper approach is cleanest:

Create `packages/server/src/llm/streaming.ts` with:

```ts
export interface StreamChunk {
  /** 分片属于哪一次调用：purpose + 一个稳定的序号 */
  channel: string;
  stage: string;
  kind: 'reasoning' | 'text';
  text: string;
}

/** 把 complete() 实现成「内部走 stream()，把增量转给监听者，最后返回完整结果」的包装客户端 */
export function withStreaming(inner: LlmClient, onChunk: (chunk) => void): LlmClient
```

But the channel needs to distinguish concurrent calls. `purpose` alone isn't enough for probe (3 interviewers, same purpose 'probe'). We need to include something identifying. Options:
- Attach the channel to the request. Add optional `channel?: string` to `LlmRequest`? That would be a field not part of digest (shapeOf only picks system/messages/temperature/maxTokens/json), so safe. But that pollutes the LLM contract.
- Alternative: the wrapper assigns each call an incrementing index within the purpose: `${purpose}#${n}`. That's stable given deterministic ordering? Concurrency means the order in which calls *start* is deterministic (Promise.all map order is deterministic in starting), so index assignment at call time is deterministic. Good. But the frontend doesn't know which probe/claim each index corresponds to.

Better: give the stages the ability to label the call. The cleanest minimal change: add optional `channel?: string` to `LlmRequest`, set in `stageRequest` via an extra optional param. Then stages pass e.g. `stageRequest('probe', system, user, 'probe:' + contract.id)`.

Wait, but then `LlmRequest.channel` — must not enter digest. `shapeOf` picks specific fields, so it won't. Let me check `shapeOf` in cassette.ts. From summary: `shapeOf` only takes system/messages/temperature/maxTokens/json. Good.

Hmm, but is adding a field to LlmRequest the right call? It's a display label like `purpose`. Purpose exists already: "人类可读的用途标签". So adding `channel` as "人类可读的路由标签" is consistent. Good — and document that it doesn't participate in the digest.

Then `withStreaming(inner, onChunk)`:
```ts
export function withStreaming(inner: LlmClient, onChunk: StreamObserver): LlmClient {
  return {
    mode: inner.mode,
    async complete(req) {
      const channel = req.channel ?? req.purpose;
      for await (const ev of inner.stream(req)) {
        if (ev.type === 'delta') onChunk({channel, stage: req.purpose, kind:'text', text: ev.text});
        else if (ev.type === 'reasoning') onChunk({channel, stage: req.purpose, kind:'reasoning', text: ev.text});
        else return ev.result;
      }
      throw new LlmError('bad-request', '流式调用没有给出最终结果：' + req.purpose);
    },
    stream(req) { return inner.stream(req); },
  };
}
```

Wait — careful: if inner.stream never yields done, throw. Fine.

But there's an important subtlety: switching all stages to internally use `stream()` in **replay** mode. Replay's stream reads the same cassette and uses chunkText. Fine, results identical. In **live** mode, stream uses the SSE endpoint. In record mode, `createLiveClient` with recordDir — stream path writes cassette with `chunks`. That's actually better.

But one risk: existing cassettes recorded via complete() have no `chunks`; replay falls back to chunkText. Fine.

Another risk: live streaming may have bugs (untested with real API). We already tested llm-live.test.ts stream path with a fake fetch. And I'll run a live test with the key.

Now, should streaming be always on or opt-in? Options:
- Always wrap: simplest, and makes SSE always stream. But `?stream=0` JSON path also streams internally (harmless, just extra work; no observer). Actually if there's no observer, we should keep `complete()` to avoid overhead. So: `withStreaming` only when onStream provided.

<details><summary>🧠 思考过程</summary>

Now I have everything I need. Let me design the streaming implementation.

Design:
1. Add to `types.ts`: a `channel` (or `label`) concept? Better: use `purpose` + a per-call `stream` id. But how does the frontend know which probe/claim a chunk belongs to? We need a channel key.

Approach: Add a `streamKey` field to `LlmRequest`? No — LlmRequest participates in cassette digest via `shapeOf` which only takes system/messages/temperature/maxTokens/json. Adding a field to LlmRequest is safe (not part of digest), but requires threading it through `stageRequest`.

Actually a cleaner approach given the concurrency: since each stage call is made inside `Promise.all`, I need each call's deltas tagged with a channel. Channel IDs:
- extract: `extract`
- probe: `probe:<interviewerId>`
- answer: `answer:<probeId>`
- verdict: `verdict:<claimId>`

4. Server-side: where do we get `onStream` into the stage functions? `runSession` creates the client... Actually stages take `client` in their input. Simplest: wrap the client in a "streaming proxy" that implements `complete` by consuming `stream()` and forwarding deltas with the channel from `req.channel`. That way NO stage code changes at all — only `withStreaming(client, onStream)` wrapper. Very clean!

```ts
export function withStreaming(inner: LlmClient, onChunk: (c: StreamChunk) => void | Promise<void>): LlmClient {
  return {
    mode: inner.mode,
    async complete(req) {
      let last: LlmResult | null = null;
      for await (const ev of inner.stream(req)) {
        if (ev.type === 'done') last = ev.result;
        else await onChunk({ channel: req.channel ?? req.purpose, stage: req.purpose, kind: ev.type, text: ev.text });
      }
      if (last === null) throw new LlmError('bad-request', '流式调用没有给出最终结果：' + req.purpose);
      return last;
    },
    stream(req) { return inner.stream(req); },
  };
}
```

Then create streaming.ts:

```ts
/**
 * 把普通的 complete() 调用变成"边生成边外推"的流式调用。
 *
 * 为什么做成客户端包装而不是改各阶段的代码：
 * 五个阶段的并发结构不一样（probe 按面试官并发、answer 按追问并发……），
 * 如果每个阶段自己写 for await，就要把并发、异常、用量累加各写五遍。
 * 包装一层之后，阶段代码一个字都不用动：它照旧 await complete()，
 * 而这一层负责把 inner.stream() 的分片按 channel 外推，并返回 done 里的最终结果。
 *
 * 三条纪律：
 * 1. 分片是**过程**，不进事件表也不落库；落库的是各阶段的结构化结果。
 * 2. 最终结果一律取自 done 事件，绝不用分片拼一个"看起来差不多"的结果——
 *    拼出来的文本可能缺尾，而下游还要过 zod。
 * 3. 没有 done 就是坏调用，明确抛错，不返回半成品。
 */
```

Then:
```ts
import type { LlmClient, LlmRequest, LlmResult } from './types.ts';
import { LlmError } from './types.ts';

export interface StreamChunk {
  /** 路由标识：extract / probe:<面试官> / answer:<追问编号> / verdict:<主张编号> / suggest:<主张编号> */
  channel: string;
  /** 阶段名，等于请求的 purpose */
  stage: string;
  /** reasoning = 模型思考过程；text = 正式输出（本项目里是结构化 JSON） */
  kind: 'reasoning' | 'text';
  text: string;
}

export type StreamSink = (chunk: StreamChunk) => void | Promise<void>;

export function withStreaming(inner: LlmClient, sink: StreamSink): LlmClient {
  return {
    mode: inner.mode,
    async complete(req: LlmRequest): Promise<LlmResult> {
      const channel = req.channel ?? req.purpose;
      let done: LlmResult | null = null;
      for await (const event of inner.stream(req)) {
        if (event.type === 'done') {
          done = event.result;
          continue;
        }
        await sink({ channel, stage: req.purpose, kind: event.type === 'reasoning' ? 'reasoning' : 'text', text: event.text });
      }
      if (done === null) {
        throw new LlmError('bad-request', '流式调用没有给出最终结果：' + req.purpose);
      }
      return done;
    },
    stream(req: LlmRequest) {
      return inner.stream(req);
    },
  };
}
```

Wait: `event.type === 'reasoning' ? 'reasoning' : 'text'` — the remaining type is 'delta'. Good.
